(292) And this, by God, to whom it is due, happens with justice, because someone has abandoned God, saints, fasts, and prayers there, and has had recourse to the sorcerer, who cannot even help himself! For which (293) of the sorcerers can be without pain, without a demon, and immortal? There is more; we see, in the reality of things, that sorcerers are always reduced to exhaustion by demons; and especially the frenzied <are exhausted> (294). For first they themselves are possessed by the demon, and then to others, such a thing, (to others,) such (another) thing, they promise to "give," which cannot be in the hands of anyone—neither in theirs, nor in those of the demons, in whose language they speak of "giving" something to someone—except (in the hands) of God alone, who is the creator and the "giver."
Նւ արդ քան անդէն կապել կախարդացն զդեւն՝ որպէս ասենն, եւ խեղդ ընդ անձն միշտ Հոգւոյն զմարդուն առնել, լաւ էր թէ յայտնէր, եւ սրբովք աղաչեցեալ զԱստուած՝ աւգնականութիւն՝ գտանէր բուժելով չարէն
And so, rather than the sorcerers "binding" the demon again, as they say (295), and [87] making of it a snare for the soul (296) of man (which it will always have) around itself, it would be better that (the demon) manifested itself, and (the man), through the intercession of the Saints and prayer made to God, would find help to be healed of the evil (297).


[p. 586] {#po.28.p586}
Այլ մեք ասեմք, թէ մտանել ի մարդ չունի իշխանութիւն՝ առանց Աստուծոյ թուլացուցանելոյն Եւ այնպէս պատճառք են, որպէս ինքն միայն գիտէ. եւ այն անտի է՝ զի զորժամ ի խոզից երամակն մտանել մտածէին. մտանել, ոչ կարացին մտանել՝ եթէ ոչ նախ ի Քրիստոսէ առին Հրաման Եւ զորժամ զՅովբն կամեցաւ փորձել սատանայ, ոչ իշխեաց մտանել՝ եթէ ոչ նախ յԱստուծոյ առ Հրաման փորձելոյ Եւ զՅուդայէ՝ ասէ՝ թէ ընդ պատառին մտաւ սատանայ ։ Զի թէ չէր Քրիստոսի թուլացուցեալ եւ սատանայի՝ որ զգրգռէրն, եւ Յուդայի՝ որ վասն կծծութեան եկն այն, ոչ իշխէր մտանել ի նա ։ Այլ վասն ցուցանելոյ զանձնիշխանութիւն երկոցունց՝ թուլացոյց ըստ կամաց երկոցունց
Conclusion to a and to B. We, on the contrary, say that it has no power to enter into man without the permission of God. And, of this (permission) there are different reasons, which He alone, in their difference (298), knows; and (this), evidently, emerges from the fact that, when they imagined entering into the herd of swine, they could not enter until they had first received an order from Christ (299). And when Satan wanted to tempt Job, he did not have the audacity to dare until he had first received an order from God to tempt (300). And of Judas, he (John) says: "Satan entered with the morsel." For, if Christ had not permitted it: both Satan, who was precisely inciting, and Judas, who had arrived at that point through avarice, would not have had the audacity to enter into him. Quite the contrary, to prove (by refutation) the free will of both, he allowed it to happen according to the will of both.
Նա եւ մինչ դեռ տանջին իսկ ոմանք ի դիւէ, եթէ ոչ յԱստուծոյ տեսչութիւն լինէր նմա, չարաչար զմարդիկ սատակէր Նա եւ զպաշտաւնեայսն իսկ իւրեանց՝ եթէ ոչ Աստուծոյ սաստ պահէր, ազգի ազգի խեղդուխեամբք խեղդէին, եւ աննկարելի մահուամբք սպա անէին ։ Բայց վասն զի ոչ են ձեռնՀաս, ոչ իշխեն այնպիսի իրս մտածել Եւ այն այն անտի է, զի թէպէտ եւ կանխաւ գիտէ Աստուած՝ թէ ոմն կռապաշտ լինելոց է, ոմն կախարդ, եւ ոմն սպանող ոչ արգելու զնկարել զսաղմսն նոցա, եւ զփչումն Հոգւոցն նոցա, զի նորա բարերարութիւնն երեւեսցի, եւ նոքա իւրաքանչիւրն անձնիշխանութեամբն պարտաւորեսցին ։
[88] Moreover, during the very time that some are tormented by a demon, if God's surveillance were not exercised over him (the demon), he would cause them to perish by a very cruel death. Furthermore, those very people who render worship to them (the demons), were it not for God's vigilant threat (over them, the demons), they would mutilate them with all kinds of mutilations and kill them with unimaginable deaths. It is only because they (the demons) do not have long hands (302) that they do not have the audacity to conceive such designs (303). And this, evidently, emerges from the fact that, although God knows in advance that such a one will become an idolater, such a one a sorcerer, and such a one a murderer, he does not put an obstacle to the sketching of their embryos, nor to the insufflation of their souls, so that his beneficence may be clearly visible, and that they, each in the play of his free
Եւ այնպէս է՝ թէ որպէս առնելն ինքն տէր է, նոյնպէս եւ ի դարմանսն աւգտելոյ, եւ ի վնասուց չարին ապրեցուցանելոյ. զի ո՛ր իւր ինչ ոք է՝ իւրն զգուշանայ եւ խնայէ, եւ որ ոչ է իւր, նա զաւտարին աւգտէ եւ ցրուէ ։ Որպէս ասէ Յիսուս ի աւետարանին՝ թէ զգայլ ոչ վասն այլ իրիք գայ, այլ զի կափշտակեսցէ եւ ցրուեսցէ
will, find the cause of their condemnation. And it is evident that, just as he is master of creating, so is he, in the remedies (for evil) (304), master of making profit found, and of making one escape safely from the vexations of the Evil One (305). For he whose thing it is, has care and concern for what is "his"; and for whom it is not his, that one tears and destroys what belongs to another. As he (Jesus) says in the Gospel: [89] "The wolf comes for no other reason than to plunder and scatter."


[p. 587] {#po.28.p587}
Այլ ճշմարիտ Հաւատացելոց ոչ սատանայ փորձութեամբքն կարէ ստա նել, եւ ոչ կախարդք դիւաւք. որպէս ինքն Տէրն ասաց ցիւր աշակերտսն՝ եթէ ահա ետու ձեզ իշխանութիւն կոխել զօձս եւ զկարիճս, եւ զաւրութիւնն զթշնամւոյն ։ Եւ դարձեալ թե նշանք Հաւատացելոց այս են. դեւս հանիցեն, ե օձս ի ձեռն առցեն, ե թե մահու ըմպիցեն, ոչ վնասիցէ:
Moreover, against true believers, neither can Satan prevail through temptations, nor sorcerers with the help of demons; as the Lord himself said to his disciples: "Behold, I have given you power to tread under foot serpents and scorpions, and all the power of the enemy." And again: "These are the miracles of believers: they will go about casting out demons, and they will take up serpents in their hands, and if they drink any deadly poison, it will not happen that it does them harm." Conclusion to c with a reminder of B' of a, of a. To him who is without sin
Եւ միանգամայն իսկ ասացեալ, թե անմեղի ոչինչ ոչ դեւք վնասել` կարեն, ե ոչ գազանք սպանանել կարեն, որպես ոչ Դանիելի գազանքն վնասեցին, ե ոչ երից մանկանցն Հուր հնոցին. որպես առաջնորդ մարդուն` մինչ չեւ անցուցեալ` նմա Հնազանդ էին, ե ոչ մեղանչականք. ե որպես առ առաքե- լովբն վատխարեալ էր սատանայ, մինչեւ կախարդացն այն նշանացն՝ զոր առնեին՝ բերել զգիրս կախարդանացն վաճառելոց՝ ե այրել առաջի առաքելոցն, ե դիւան աղաղակել ` թե դոքա են ծառայք Աստուծոյ բարձրելոյ. որոց նշանաց նշխարք՝ ն այժմ ի սուրբ եպիսկոպոսս ե ի ճշմարիտ վանականս երեւին. ե վասն դոր փորձ ոչ միայն քրիստոնեայք, այլ ն Հեթանոսք ե Հրեայք գիտեն:
neither ferocious beasts nor demons can do harm. And let it be said once and for all what the case is (307): to him who is without sin, demons cannot do harm, and ferocious beasts are not inclined to kill (him), just as ferocious beasts were not harmful to Daniel, and the fire of the furnace was not harmful to the three young men; as they were subject to the first man until he had transgressed, and were not pernicious; and as, in the time of the Apostles, Satan was then put to shame, to such an extent that, in the terror into which the miracles they performed threw them (308), sorcerers brought their books of sorcery, worth a heavy [90] sum, and burned them before the apostles, and demons cried out loudly: "These whom you have before you (309) are servants of the Most High God!" of which miracles, the remainder, even in the time we are in, is visible in holy bishops and true cenobites; and that this is experienced not only by Christians, but pagans and magi know it too. C. Pneumatology (on the occasion of diabolical possessions)
Բայց ն այս եւս պարտիմք գիտել, թե ե Հրեշտակք ե դեւք ե ոգիք մարդկան անմարմինք են: Զի Հրեշտակացն ասէ՝ արար զՀրեշտակս իւր Հոգիս, ե զպաշտաւնեայս իւր բոց Հրոյ: Հոգիս կոչէ զնոսա վասն արագութեանն, որպես թե ասիցէ՝ թե քան զհողմս թեթեւք են, քանզի ոգւորդ ե Հողմոյն անուն եբրայեցերեն ե հունարեն ե ասորերեն նոյն է. նա թե ն Հայերեն որ մանր միտ դնիցէ՝ նոյնպես գտանի: Յորժամ տագնապեալ ոք մեկուսի իցէ՝ ասէ, չետ ոգի կլանել, չետ ոգի առնուլ, ե այնու դաւթով՝ զոր միշտ ծծեմք՝ յայտ առնէ: Եւ բոցեղենս կոչէ զնոսա վասն սաստկութեանն. որպես այլուր ասէ, Տզաւրք են զաւրութեամբ առնել զկամս նորա. այլ ոչ են ի բնութենէ Հողմոյն ե Հրոյն են: Զի եթե ի բնութենէ Հողմոյն ե Հրոյ էին, ապա ն նոքա իրաւի մարմնաւորք կոչէին ե ոչ անմարմինքն: Քանզի որ մարմնաւոր է՝ ի չորից
a. ANGELS, DEMONS, SOULS OF MEN: INCORPOREAL [I. XXIII] Only, this too, again, we must know: that angels and demons and breaths (souls) of men are incorporeal. For, of the Angels (Scripture) says: "He made his Angels, breaths (310), And his servants, a flame of fire." He calls them "breaths" for their velocity, as if wanting to say that they are lighter than winds; for the name of "breath" and "wind," in Hebrew and in Greek and in Syriac, is the same. Now, even in Armenian, if one is willing to pay a little attention to it, so it is found. When someone happens to have been strongly pressed by someone else, he says: "He did not let (me) swallow breath!", "He did not let (me) take breath!", and, by that, it is obviously this air that we are always inhaling that he means to speak of. And it calls them "beings of flame" for their vehemence, as elsewhere it [91] says: "They are powerful, to do his will with power," but they do not, for all that, derive their being from the nature of wind and fire.


[p. 588] {#po.28.p588}
նիւթից կազմեալ է, որպես մարմինք մարդկան ե բնաւ անասնոց. ե որ ինչ անմարմին է՝ նա պարզ բնութիւն է, որպես Հրեշտակաց ե դիւաց ե ոգւոցն մարդկան:
For if they derived their being from the nature of wind and fire, consequently, they too would rightly be called corporeal and not incorporeal. For that which is corporeal is composed of four materials, like the bodies of men and, in their totality, those of animals; and all that is incorporeal is a simple nature, like the (nature) of angels and demons and breaths (souls)
Եւ արդ ոքր իշխիցեն շշնջել, որք ասեն` եթե Հրեշտակք ընդ կանանց ամուսնացան, զի նոքա Հրեղենք կոչին, ե մարդիկ խոտեղենք, որպես մարգարեն ասէ՝ թե ամենայն մարմին խոտ է: Ուստի այն խիստ է՝ թե ի Հրոյն խոտոյն ոչ ամուսնութիւն լինի, այլ ծախումն: Եւ երեքեան դասքս այսոքիկ, քանզի Համաբնակք են, նշանագոյնս ե նովին իսկ անուամբ անուանին:
of men. And so where will they have the audacity to go whispering, those who say that Angels married women? For those are called "beings of fire," and men "herbaceous," as the prophet says: "All flesh is grass." Whence it is very clear that no marriage takes place between fire and grass, but rather consumption (of the latter). And, all together, these three groups that occupy us (311), because they are of the same nature, are likewise named by exactly the same name.
Ասի Հրեշտակ ոգի, այլ ոգի պաշտաւոր՝ որ է Հնազանդ ե կամակատար: Ասիւ է դեւ ոգի, այլ ոգի չար վասն անՀնազանդութեանն ե ապստամբ- ութեանն. որ թեպետ ն ի մեր լեզուի՝ այս չար ասեմք, որպես ի խնդրանաց` Հարցն մերոց առաջնոյ, ըստ սովորութեան ի մեր կարգելոյն այլ գիտեմք՝ թե այն Հողմ է, ե Հողմն՝ ոգի, ըստ առաջագոյնն ասացելոյն: Քանզի յորժամ մեք ասեմք՝ թե սիւք շնչէ, ասորիներն ասեն՝ այս շնչէ. է. վասն տարածութեանն միայն է. արագութեան կոչին Հրեշտակք ե դեւք ե ոգիք մարդկան՝ ոդեղենք, որ է Հողմեղենք: Որպես վասն սաստկութեանն բոցեղեն կոչին Հրեշտակք, նոյնպես ն վասն արագութեանն ե տարածութեան Հոգեղենք, որ է Հողմեղենք. բայց բնութիւն նոցա ի վեր է քան զՀողմ ե քան զՀուր, ե նուրբ է արագ քան զմիտս:
An Angel is called a "breath (spirit)," but a breath (spirit) in commanded service, that is to say, in submission and as an executor of wills. A Demon is also called a "breath (spirit)," but an evil breath (spirit), for its insubordination and its rebellion; which (evil breath, spirit), [92] although in our language we call it a bad "puff"—as one might say by virtue of the observances of our first fathers, according to a habit established among us—yet we know well that the "puff" is "wind," and the "wind," "breath" (spirit), in accordance with what was said previously. For, when we say: "Breeze breath," the people of the south (i.e., the Syrians) say "Puff breath"; and it is only for the extent (of their range of action) and velocity that Angels and demons and souls (breaths) of men are called "beings made of air," that is to say, "beings made of wind." As, for their vehemence, Angels are called "beings of flame," likewise also, for their velocity and the extent (of their range of action), they are called "beings made of breath," that is to say, "beings made of wind." Apart from that, their nature is above wind
Եւ չեն ինչ գարշանք՝ թե նոքա մերոց դրացեայ արարածոց անուամբք անուանին, ուր ե նոցա արարիչն ոչ գարշի այնպիսի անուանս ինքեամբ առնուլ՝ վասն իրիք իրիք տեսչութեանց: Կոչի Աստուած Հոգի, այլ կայ ընտրութիւն ի միջի: Ասէ, Աստուած Հոգի կենդանի է. որ է կենարար: Կոչի ե Հուր. ըստ այնմ՝ թե Աստուած քո Հուր ծախիչ է:
and fire, subtle and swifter than thought (312). And there is no astonishment to be had if those (the Angels) are named (in Scripture) by these names, which are those of creatures neighboring us, where even the Creator of them has no repugnance to clothing himself (313) with names of this kind, because of such or such views of Providence (314). God is called: "Breath," but discernment must be made here [93]. (Scripture) says: "God is living breath," that is to say, "life-giving." He is also called "fire," according to this (text): "Your God is a consuming fire."
Եւ արդ տեսցուք՝ թե Հուր միայն ծախիչ իցէ Աստուած: Նա աւանիկ նորին Հոգւով մարգարէիւ յայտ արարեալ՝ թե լոյս է. ըստ այնմ՝ թե Տէր լոյս իմ է. կեանք իմ, ես ումեք երկեալցն:
And so, let us see if God would be only a "consuming fire." But behold, the same Spirit (Holy), through another prophet, revealed that he


[p. 589] {#po.28.p589}
Եթե Հուր ծախիչ եւեթ էր, զինչ կոչեր լոյս կենարար: Ոչ ապաքեն յայտ է՝ թե ուր սաստկութիւնն պիտոյ է, անդ Հուր կոչի, ե ուր քաղցրութիւն՝ անդ լոյս կենարար: Այլ ինքն ի վեր է քան զՀուր ե քան զ լոյս. ն այլ ինչ բազում անուանս առնու ղինքեամբ վասն պեսպես դար- ձանից:
is "light"; according to this (text): "The Lord is my light and my life, of whom shall I have to fear?" If he were only a "consuming fire," how would he be called "life-giving light"? Is it not in truth evident that there, where vehemence is needed, there he is called "fire"; and there, where sweetness is needed, there, "life-giving light"? But [315] he himself is above fire and above light; and he clothes himself with all sorts of other names, very numerous, for different salutary ends.
Եւ որժամ երեւել սրբոց իւրոց կամեր, ոչ այլով իւիք կերպարանաւք՝ այլ միայն մարդոյ կերպարանաւք, զոր ըստ իւրում պատկերին արար, յայտնէր։ Եւ այլ ոչ վայրապար ինչ լինէր, այլ զի զառաւել սերն իւր՝ որ ընդ մարդ կան ուներ՝ ցուցանիցէ։ Եւ դարձեալ ի առաջագոյն կրթեսցէ զմարդիկ ածել ի դիտութիւն. զի, որժամ զորդին իւր իբրեւ մարդ աշխարհ առաքեսցէ, մի աւտարոտի ինչ վերսն Հաճարեսցին. մանաւանդ, զի գիտիցեն՝ թէ ինքն իսկ այն կերպարանաւք յայտնէր։ Որպէս իջեալ ի դրախտն առ Ադամ՝ ոտնաձայն իբրեւ զմարդ առնէր։ Եւ եկեալ Որդւոյն առ Աբրաամ երկուց Հրեշտակաւք, արժանի առնէր ճաշ ի վրանի նորա ուտելոյ։ Եւ ի խաւս մատուցեալ ի լերինն ընդ Աբրահամու, երկուս պատանիս իւր՝ զՀրեշտակս մարդկակերպեալս՝ ի Սոդոմ առ դեղեցիկ ասպետականն առ Ղաւտ արձակէր։ Եւ Հրացուցեալ եւ չալալեալ զՀրեշտակն ի թփենւոջն, ի մարդկեղեն բարբառ ածեալ, տայր ընդ նախընծայելոյն՝ առնն Աստուծոյն ընդ Մովսէսի խաւսել։ Եւ մարդակերպեալ զզաւրավարն իւր զՄիքայէլ, զաւրագլխին իւրում Յեսուայ ի դաշտին ցուցանէր։ Եւ առաքեալ Հրեշտակ ի տուն Մանուէի խաւսել ընդ նմա, մարդ- կաւրէն ընծայեցուցանէր։
And when he wanted to appear to his saints, he manifested himself in no other forms, but only with the forms of man, which he made in his image. And this was not at all without reason, but to the end that he might show the excess of love that he bore for [94] man. And, in the second place, to exercise men in advance, in order to lead them into knowledge; so that, when he would have sent his own Son into the world as a man, they would not go, in the least, to find the thing strange; but rather so that they would know that it was he himself in person who was manifesting himself under these forms. As, once descended into paradise to meet Adam, he made (heard) a sound of footsteps, just like a man. And the Son, once come to Abraham with two Angels, deigned to eat a meal in the tent of the latter (316 bis). And, having entered into conversation on the mountain with Abraham, he sent his two young men, the Angels, in human form, to Sodom, to the handsome host, to Lot. And, once he had rendered fire, without altering its nature, he made the Angel in the bush, once he had brought him to take on a human voice, converse with the man of God, once the latter had presented himself first (317), with Moses. And, once he had made the general of his armies, Michael, take human form, he showed him to the captain of his armies, Joshua, in the plain. And, once he had [95] sent an Angel into the house of Manoah, to speak with him, it was in a human form that he presented him (to him).
Եւ երբեք երբեք գրեթէ մարդացեալ իսկ՝ բերան ի բերան, ձեռն ի ձեռն ընդ բարեկամին իւրում ընդ Մովսէսի խաւսէր։ Եւ առնն թագաւորի ըստ սրտի իւրում գտելոյն, թախծելոյ ի հարուա- ծոցն, Հրեշտակ ի կերպարանս մարդոյն սուսեր ի ձեռն ցուցանէր։ Եւ Եզեկիէլի Հրատեսիլ եւ բոցալեզու ի կառս, ազգի ազգի կերպարանաւք յաւդեալ, եւ կառավար մարդկակերպարանաւ երեւեցուցանէր. եւ քերովբէս ազգի ազգի դիւաց կերպարանաւք յաւդեալ, եւ ձեռն ձգեալ իբրեւ մարդոյն քերովբէի ի
And many and many a time, almost having become a man for good, he spoke mouth to mouth, hand to hand, with his friend, with Moses. And to the man, the king who was found to be according to his heart, dismayed under the blows (of a scourge), he showed an Angel in the form of a man with a sword in his hand. And to Ezekiel, with a face of fire and a tongue of flame, in a chariot composed of various figures, he made a chariot driver also appear in human form, and he showed him cherubim composed of


[p. 590] {#po.28.p590}
միջոյ քերովբէից ցուցանէր։ Եւ ինքն իսկ երբեմն իբրեւ զհինաւրեալն, երբեմն իբրեւ զմատաղաւրեալն, վասն այլ եւ այլ տեսչութեանց կերպարանեալ՝ ցանկալւոյն առնն երեւէր։ Եւ այնպէս իւր եւ իւրոց Հառալիցն նոյնգունակ երեւելով՝ զառաւել սիրոյն, զոր ընդ մարդոյն ուներ, յայտ առնէր։
various beasts, and a hand of a cherub, like that of a man which, stretched out, came out from the midst of the cherubim (318). And he himself in person, sometimes as an Ancient of days, sometimes as a man in his tender youth, for different views of providence, having taken the form, appeared to the man of desire (319). And thus, by his own apparitions and those of his servants, under one and the same color (human) (320), he manifested the excess of love that he bore for man.
Այս ամենայն ասացաւն, զի ցուցցի թէ որ ինչ երեւի՝ մարմնաւոր է, եւ որ ինչ ոչ երեւի՝ անմարմին։ Եւ ի մարմնաւորացն են որ թանձրամարմինք են, եւ են որ նուրբամար- մինք. որպէս Առաքեալ ասէ՝ թէ այլ մարմինք երկնաւորացն են, եւ այլ մարմինք երկրաւորացս։ Երկրաւորաց՝ մարդկան եւ անասնոց, թռչնոց եւ սողնոց. եւ երկնաւորաց՝ արեգական եւ լուսնոյ եւ աստեղաց։ Եւ զի նոցանէ ասէ, եւ ոչ զՀրեշտակացն, անդէն դիւիմբ յաւդեալ ասէ՝ թէ այլ փառք արեգական են, եւ այլ փառք լուսնի։ Եւ ի Հանդամայն իսկ ասացեալ՝ թէ որ ինչ ի զգալեացս շաւշափի կամ գտանի կամ ազդի՝ այն մարմնաւոր է, եւ որ՝ զգալեաց ոչ ազդի՝ անմարմին։ Նուրբ է տարր լուսոյն, այլ զի ակամբ լննի՝ մարմնաւոր է. նուրբ է տարր աւդոյն, այլ ցրտութեամբն ազդի մարմնոյն՝ մարմնաւոր է. նուրբ է տարր Հրոյն, այլ զի ջերմութեամբն ազդի մարմնոյն՝ մարմնաւոր է. նոյնպէս եւ ջրոյն՝ որ քան Ալկետսն նրբագոյն է, եւ քան զթեթեւսն թանձրագոյն։
Conclusion to a. [96] All this has been said so that it might be demonstrated that everything that is visible is corporeal, and everything that is not visible is incorporeal. And among corporeal (beings), there are those that are of dense corporeality, and there are those that (are) of rarefied corporeality. As the Apostle says: "Other are the bodies of celestial (beings), and other the bodies of terrestrial (beings) here below." "Of terrestrial (beings)": (that is to say) of men and animals, birds and reptiles; and "of celestial (beings)": (that is to say) of the sun and the moon and the stars. And that it is indeed of those that he says (it), and not of the Angels, in the same place, by encompassing it in the same (sentence), he says (it): "Other is the glory of the sun, and other the glory of the moon" (321). And, speaking absolutely in general, everything that is felt or perceived or sensed by our senses is corporeal; and that which does not make itself felt to the senses is incorporeal. The element of light is thin, but because it is perceived by the eye, it is corporeal; the element of air is thin, but (322), by its coldness, it makes itself felt to the body, it is corporeal; the element of fire is thin, but because, by its heat, it makes itself felt to the body, [97] it is corporeal. Likewise also of (the element of) water, which is thinner than [the whales] (323), and denser than light (bodies). b. ANGELS AND DEMONS, being incorporeal, do not procreate: non-existence
[I. Եւ արդ քանզի անմարմինք են Հրեշտակք եւ դեւք, այն սակաւ եւ ծնունդք ոչ գոն նոցա։ Այլ, ասեն, Համբարուաց ծնունդք են եւ մեռանին։ Նախ այն տեսցուք, թե բնաւ ղուցեն այլ ինչ արարածք բանաւորք քան զՀրեշտակս եւ զդեւս եւ զմարդիկ։ Եւ ապա եկեսցուք յայլեր ի բնին, թե իցեն ի դիւաց կէսք մարմնաւորք եւ կէսք անմարմինք։ Զի չիք ինչ արարած բանաւոր արտաքս քան զայս երիս ջոկս, զՀրեշտակաց,
of centaurs, fairies, sirens, and other malevolent chimerical beings. [I. XXIV] And so, because Angels and demons are incorporeal, for this reason, births do not exist among them either. But, they say, among fairies, there are births, and they die. First, let us see this: does there exist at all any other kind of reasonable creature, other than Angels and demons and men? Then after, we will come to the examination of this (question): among demons, can there be some that are corporeal and some that are incorporeal? That there is absolutely no reasonable creature outside of these three
զդիւաց եւ զմարդկան, յամենայն աստուածատուր գրոցն եւ ի բնութենէ արարածոց յայտնի է։
groups: that of the Angels, that of the demons, and that of men, from all the Scriptures given by God and from the nature of creatures, this emerges to
Բայց թէպէտ եւ ասին ինչ անուանք՝ ի գրոց յուշկապարիկաց կամ Համբարուացն կամ պարիկաց, յատ կարծեաց մտաց մարդկան ասին, եւ ոչ յատ բնութեան։ Քանզի դիւաց է կերպարանս կերպարանս ցուցանել, եւ մարդիկ յատ կերպարանացն անուանս դնեն. Որպէս որժամ բաղաքք եւ դեւք աւերեսցին, եւ անդ դեւք բնակիցեն, եւ կեղծս ի կեղծս յայտնիցին, եւ մարդ- կան ըստ կեղծեաց նոցա անուանս եղեալ՝ զոմն յուշկապարիկ, ոմն պարիկ, զոմն Հաճրարու կոչիցեն. նոյնպէս եւ դրոց ըստ մարդկան կարծեացն եկեալ՝ առ ի նշանակելո զսաստկութիւնն աւերակի աշխարհին, զյուշկապարիկաց ասեն բնակել յաւերակսն, զոր հուն լեզու իշացուլս ասէ։ Արդ յուցցեն թե գտանիցին իշացուլք ի Բաբելոնի. ապա ուրեմն յայտ է՝ երե անուանքն առանց անձանց են յուշկապարիկաց եւ իշացլուց. եւ դիրթ յատ սովորութեան մարդկան կարծեաց եկեալ, նոյնպէս անուանեցին՝ առ ի զաւերն Բաբելոնի նշանակելոյ, իբրեւ ծովացուլն՝ որ ի կովէ ելեալ ասեն, եւ զպանն ինն ի մարդկանէ, եւ զառլելն ի շանէ։ Եւ այլեւ ոչ երե անձինք ինչ են, այլ անուանք զբանդտանաց, եւ բամբա[99]սանքն մոլորելոց։ Զի ի մարմնաւորէ աներեւոյթ ինչ ոչ ելանէ, որպէս ոչ աներեւութէ ինչ մարմնաւոր։ Ոչ երբեք ի մարդկանէ ելեալ սատիրք արածիցեն, եւ ոչ ի կովուց ծովացուլն ելեալ՝ եթէ ի ծովակս բնակիցէ։ Զի մարմնաւորի՝ չէ հնար՝ թե ի ջուրս կեցցէ, որպէս ոչ ջրականին՝ թե ի ցամաքի կեցցէ. եւ ոչ ի շանէ ինչ ելեալ՝ թե ընդ աներեւոյթ զաւրութիւնս ինչ կեցցէ, եւ որժամ վիրաւոր որ անկեալ ի պատերազմի ղեիցի՝ թե լեզցէ եւ ողջացուցանիցէ. այլ ամենայնն՝ առասպելք են, եւ պառաւաց դրդջիւնք, եւ մանաւանդ թե ի դիւաց իսկ մոլորութիւնք։
the evidence. Only, although names of centaurs, or fairies, or sirens are said in a certain way (324) by Scripture, it is according to the ideas that the mind of men had of them that they are said (by it), and not according to nature. For it is the property of demons to show forms and forms, and men, according to the [98] forms, impose names. In the way that, when cities and villages have been ruined and demons come to dwell there, and they come to manifest themselves under a thousand simulated forms, men, for their part (325), once they have imposed names according to the forms that (the demons) simulate, go on to call one (of these forms) a centaur, another a siren, another a fairy; in the same way, Scripture, too, by condescension (326) to what men imagine, to describe with what violence the ruin of the country (327) was consumed, speaks of centaurs, that they make their dwelling in the ruins; (beings) that the Greek language calls: donkey-bulls (328). Well! (329) will they show that donkey-bulls can be found in Babylon? Consequently, it is therefore evident that "names," without persons (thereby designated) (330), are (these names) of centaurs and donkey-bulls; and Scripture, by condescension (326) to what men are accustomed to imagine, has employed the same names (as them) to describe the ruin of Babylon... (331), like the sea-bull that is said to be born of a cow, and a species of satyr, born of men, and the "Take!-Lick!," born of a dog (332). And this (333), not that these are in any way "persons," but "names" of fabulous (beings) (334), and babble of spirits led astray by demons. For from a corporeal (being), no invisible (being) comes out; just as from no invisible (being) does a corporeal (being) come out. Never has a satyr come out of men that he should go [99] to pasture wild beasts; and the sea-bull has not come out of cows, that he should go to make his dwelling in the small seas (lakes). Because it is not possible for a corporeal (being) to go live in the waters, just as it is not possible for an aquatic (being) to go live on dry land; and no (being) has come out of a dog, that he should go live with I know not what invisible Powers, and, when a wounded man fallen in a combat comes to be presented to him (336), that he should go to lick and heal (him). But all this is fables and old wives' tales, and above all, this comes from the wanderings into which demons lead [I. XXV] But, and still contesting, they hold firm to their affirmations. One says: "In our village, one cow gave birth to sea-bulls (337), and their bellowing, we all hear it continually." And the other says: "Pan (335), in person, with my own eyes, I saw him!"


[p. 591] {#po.28.p591}
ԱՅԼ ԵՒ վիճեալ եւս պնդին ի բանիցն վերայ։ Մին ասէ՝ թե ի մերում դեաւղ ծովացուլս կովն գործեաց, եւ զգոչիւնն հանապազ մեք լսեմք ամենեքեան։ Եւ միւսն ասէ. զՊանն իսկ իմ աչաւք տեսեալ եմ։
to lick and heal (it). But all this is fables and old wives' tales, and above all, it comes from the wanderings into which demons lead [I. XXV] But, still contesting, they hold firm to their assertions. One says: "In our village, a cow produced sea-bulls (337), and their bellowing, continuously, we all hear it." And the other says: "Pan (335), in person, with my own eyes, I have seen him!"
Միթե եւ Վերալեզն եւս ոք կարիցէ ասել՝ թե եւ զնա ոք աչաւք տեսեալ իցէ։ Եւ եթե յառաջին ժամանակս Վերալեզք լեզէին վիրա ւորս եւ ողջացուցանէին, արդ ընդեր ոչ լեզցեն եւ ողջացուցանիցեն։ Ոչ նոյն պատերազմունք են, եւ նոյնպէս վերաւորք անկանին։
[100] Could someone say that someone else also saw the "Take!-Lick!" (338)? And if, in the early times, "Take-Licks" licked the wounded and healed them, why do they not come to lick and heal now? Are these not the same wars, and do the wounded not fall in the same way?


[p. 592] {#po.28.p592}
«Այլ անժամ, ասեն, դիւցազունք էին։
But in that time, they say, heroes existed, who were of the race of the gods.
Ն. մեք զդիցն իսկ պահանջեսցուք ի նոցանէ։ Մարմնաւորք էին արդ- եաւք իքն, թե անմարմինք»։ Եթե մարմնաւոր էին, յայտն է՝ եթե մարդիկ էին, եւ մարդկան ուրուա. կանս ի պաշտաւն առեալ՝ դից անուանեն։ Ապա եթե անմարմինք էին, չէր կարի անմարմնոցն ընդ կանայս մարմնաւորս ամուսնանալ։ Զի եթե այլեւս հնար ինչ էր, ոչ երբեք դադարէր սատանայն ի կանաց սատանայածինս հնարել։ Զի որ անմարմին ոք է, նա եւ անսերմն է. վասն զի սերմն մարմնաւորաց է, եւ ոչ անմարմնոց. եւ առանց սերման ի կնոջէ հնանել միումն միայն կարի եղեւ՝ որ արարիչն է բնութեան մարմնոյ. եւ որպէս կամեցաւ, կարող եղեւ ի կուսէ հնանել առանց ամուսնութեան։ Ուստի ամենայն իմաստնոց արտաքնոց իւրաքանչիւր դիցն տկարութիւն հայեցեալ, չէշխեցին ասել՝ թե ի կնոջէ ոք առանց ամուսնու թեան հնաւ։
And we, on the subject of the gods precisely, we will demand (an answer) from them. Were the gods corporeal, then, or incorporeal? If they were corporeal, it is evident that they were men, and that (339), from the phantoms of men—once they have been taken as an object of worship—they are called "gods." Consequently, if they were incorporeal, it was not possible for incorporeal beings to unite by marriage with corporeal women. For if there had been any means of doing this, Satan would never have ceased to beget women, a brood of Satans. For whoever is incorporeal, that one is also without seed; because seed is proper to corporeal beings and not to incorporeal ones; and, [101] without seed, to be born of a woman, this was possible only to one alone, (to him) who is the creator of the nature of the body; and, as he willed, he had the power to be born of a Virgin, without marriage. Hence it comes that all the Sages from without (340), with eyes wide open to the impotence of their gods, taken each to each, have not had the audacity to say that any one of them was born of a woman without marriage. c. ANGELS, DEMONS, SOULS OF MEN DO NOT DIE
Եւ որպէս ոչ գոն ծնունդք դիւաց, նոյնպէս եւ ոչ մահ։ Թեպետ եւ անմահ բնութեամբ աստուածութիւնն եւեթ է՝ որն յաւիտենա ւորն է, եւ ոչ ումեքէ առեալ սկիզբն լինելոյ, այլ եւ սկզբնաւորացն եւ բանաւորաց պարգեւեալ աննախանձ զանմահութիւն, յորմէ հրեշտակաց եւ դիւաց ասեմ, եւ զոգւոյն մարդկան։ Յորոց մարդիկ, բանե լերկուց բնութեանց են՝ ի մարմնաւորաց եւ յանմարմնոց, իրաւի սերին ծննդեամբ, եւ գան յաճումն ամուսնութեամբ. եւ մեռանին, մարմնովք եւ ոչ ոգւովք, վասն պատուիրանազանցութեանն։ Այլ հրեշտակք եւ դեւք ոչ յաւելուած ծննդեամբ առնուն, եւ ոչ պակասութիւն մահուամբ. այլ որպէս հաստատեցան՝ նոյնպէս եւ նովին թուով կան, առանց յաւելուածոյ եւ նուազութեան։
And just as there are no births of demons, so also there is no death. Although only the Divinity is immortal by nature, which is (341) eternal, and has received the beginning of being from no one, yet, even to (beings) that have had a beginning, and are reasonable, he has given the gift of immortality, without jealously reserving it for himself: I speak of Angels, and demons, and the souls of men. Among these (creatures), men, because they derive from two natures—from (that) of corporeal beings and (from that) of incorporeal beings (342)—it is rightly that they propagate by birth, and go on increasing by marriage; and they die—as to the bodies and not as to the souls (343)—because of the transgression of the commandment. [102] But Angels and demons neither take increase by birth, nor do lacks occur among them by death; quite otherwise: in the way they were formally constituted, in the same way, and in the same number, they remain, without increase and (without) diminution.
Յարս են և ազդեցութիւնքն ինչ բնականք՝ որովք Հանդերձելոց իրիւք իրաց ազդ լինիցի նոցա: Որպես ձիուն՝ ի սմբակէն դնելոյ. Եւ եզինն՝ ձմերանի ընդ խորոց կողմն նստելոյ, և մօտ ի գարունն ընդ դրանց կողմն դառնալոյն. Եւ ծիծեռանն՝ առաջ քան զաշունն ի ջերմ տեղիս ի ձմերոց երթալոյ. և կռունկն՝ դիմանալ առաջագոյն ձմերունս կարեւորս, և վաղ ի մարմանդ տեղիս փութալոյ. Եւ ճայից՝ վաղագոյն տարմատարմ շրջելոյն, և աղաւնեաց՝ գամաղունդ երամ երամ խաղալոյ. Եւ ագռաւուց՝ ի ստուերակաց վաղագոյն ի ջերմ տեղիս դնալոյ. Եւ անգղց՝ ի հեռաստանէ զգիշեր անկանելոյ դիտաւորութեամբ. Եւ այլոց և ամենայն անասնոց և թռչնոց. Որպես և մրջեան՝ յամարանոյն պատրաստելոյ զկերակուր, և ընդ երկու զգարին կտրելոյ՝ զի մի բուսանիցի, և ի ջերմ ժամանակս զան ելոյ զկտիկն յորջէ անտի և ցամաքեցուցանելոյ. Եւ մեղուացն զդրունս մեղուանոցացն առաջ քան զցուրտն ականաչոմով խելոյ. Եւ արջոյ՝ առաջագոյն քան զձմեռն ի բունս մտանելոյ. Եւ երէն՝ կանուխ ի լերանց ի դաշտ դիպչելոյ. Եւ եղջերուաց՝ զժամանակ խառնիցն իմանալոյ. Եւ իշաւորաց բինացելոց՝ զարու աւանակս ի մատղաշութեան կտրելոյ. Եւ այս ամենայն ազդեցութիւնքն բնականք են յանասունսն, և ոչ խոր- հրդականք, որ յարարչէն նոցա տնկեալ են ի նոսա՝ առ ի յաւժարելոյն յաւդ- տակարն և խորշելոյ ի վնասակարացն:
(583) only, it is led toward the useful, and, by repulsion, turns away from the harmful. COROLLARIES TO 1 AND II ON WHAT IS OUTSIDE OF FREEDOM 1. Instincts of Beasts. Among them (animals) there are also certain natural instincts, thanks to which, a premonition of certain future things comes to them on occasion. As to the horse (instinct by feeling the ground) with the hoof, to know where to place its foot (584); and to the ox, in winter, to sit on the side of the hollows (of the stable), and, at the approach of spring, to return to the side of the doors (584); and to the swallow, before autumn, to go away to warm countries, to winter quarters; and to the crane, to know (585) in advance of harsh winters, and, quickly, to make haste toward grassy places; and to the jays, more quickly, in flocks, to circle; and to the wood pigeons, by aggregating, flight by flight, in a single band, to give themselves movement; and to the crows, leaving shaded places, to come as quickly as possible to warm places; and to the vultures, from very far away, in case a corpse is falling, to dart their eye there; and to others, and to all animals and birds. As to the ant also, from the summer, to prepare [175] food; and, in two, to cut the grain, so that it does not go to germinate; and, in hot times, to pull the millet from the burrow out there, and to make it dry; and to the bees, to block the doors of their hives, before the cold, with wax with holes (586); and to the bear, before winter, to enter into a den; and to the wild beast, early, leaving the mountains, to move quickly to the plain; and to the deer, at the appointed time, to know the time of mating; and to the onagers, taken with jealousy, to castrate the males among (their) young, at a tender age. And all these instinctive knowledges are natural in animals, and not at all the fruit of reflective thought; (knowledges) which, by their creator, have been implanted in them, so that they move by inclination toward the useful, and, by repulsion, move away from things that can harm them. d. THERE IS NO CREATURE THAT CAN EXIST UNDER DIFFERENT FORMS And there exists no other creature that can change itself into different forms (344), as, of dragons and crocodiles (345), they go about highly telling the story, but (there exist) only Angels and demons, who have the power to roll the air into a ball and to stretch it (346) and to make all sorts of forms appear. But, both dragons, they say, and crocodiles, change themselves into various simulated forms (344), of which one is (that of a) personal (being), and the other (that of a) non-personal (being). The dragon, which is corporeal, cannot change its forms. For if there were some means for corporeal beings to change forms, man first of all, who is greater than they (the dragons), would change his forms into whatever he wished. It is otherwise: just as for man there is no means of changing (348) into any forms whatsoever, such or such according to the whim of his will (349), likewise also there is no (means) for the dragon. [103] Furthermore: no crocodile that is (to be) personal exists; only, there is this: a demon in some places will have made its dwelling, and sometimes it comes to take form and sometimes to work harm. And dragons do not undertake to carry the products of threshing (350); and no beast of burden exists for them, namely: that they would undertake, the work done, to carry it somewhere away from the threshing floors; and it is of no use that someone, on the threshing floors, pronounces the word: "Agrippa! Agrippa!" and not: "Take! Take!" (351). For the dragon, which is itself a beast of burden, since it has neither reason nor language, how would it, which is itself a beast of burden, go and lead another beast of burden? That the specific nature of the dragon is absolutely nothing other than that of the serpent (352) is evident. And, a serpent with enormous limbs or any beast born of the sea, Scripture calls "dragon." Just as a man with enormous limbs it names "giant," in the same manner both a monstrous terrestrial serpent and the marine beast with a mountain-like form—I speak of whales and dolphins—it names dragons, in accordance with this (text): "You shattered the heads of the dragons on the waters and gave (them) as food to the peoples of the Ethiopians." [104] You see well (353) that, great fish born of the sea, Scripture calls them "dragons"! And that it speaks of fish, this emerges clearly from what it says: "You gave (them) as food to the Ethiopian nations." Although at this place other (exegetes) have, by allegory, related the dragon to Satan, as also that (354) of which Job precisely left the description. And dragons are nothing other than either very great serpents, terrestrial, or gigantic fish, marine, of which Scripture says that they have the height of a mountain, that they are very great. And their prey and food are small fish, as those of very great serpents are all sorts of tiny, tiny worms, or animalcules. And never have dragons organized hunts, as men do, or will (355) come to organize; and no palaces, as men, do they have, where to make dwelling. And no one of royal race and of the race of the gods do they keep chained up alive among them; for, of living beings who are descended from corporeal ones, there are only two: Enoch and Elijah. But, in the way that concerning Alexander, demons went about deceiving, (pretending) that he remained alive, [105] while it was they who, once they had, according to the methods of the Egyptians, chained, cast, by sorceries, a demon into a vial, made believe that Alexander was alive and dead implored — and the advent of Christ confounded the deceit and radically eliminated the scandal — in the same way also the art of seducing of demons deceived the worshippers of false gods among the Armenians, making them believe that a certain one named Artawazd, the demons had put in fetters, which (Artawazd) until now persists alive and must come out of it and take possession of this world; and, in this false hope chained persist the infidels, like the Jews, who in vain hope persist chained, (believing) that David must come to build Jerusalem, and gather the Jews, and there reign, he, over them (356). And thus Satan strives, all, whoever they may be, to deprive them of a good hope (357), and, in false hope, to enchain them. He magnifies dragons in the eyes of men, so that, when to some they have seemed terrifying beings, they (men) take them as an object of worship. [106] He makes believe that there are: certain river crocodiles, and tutelary genii of places. Then, after having made it believed, he himself transforms, either into dragon forms, or into those of any crocodile whatsoever, and into those of a tutelary genius, in order, thereby, to turn man away from his Creator. For if, in some way (358), the crocodile were a personal (being) (359), then neither (360) would it sometimes appear in the forms of a woman, nor would it sometimes become a seal, nor, once fallen on the feet of swimmers, would it suffocate (them) (under the waters); but either the woman would remain a woman, or the seal a seal. Similarly also that which they call the tutelary genius of places, neither (360) would it sometimes appear as a man, nor sometimes as a serpent, whereby also (Satan) has found a way to introduce the worship of serpents into the world. Similarly also the dragon, neither (360) would it once appear in the form of a serpent, nor another time in the form of a man; according to what has been previously formally said, namely: that everything that is corporeal cannot change into other forms (than its own). But if, on the threshing floors, mules and camels come to appear, these are forms of demons, and not of dragons. And if on [107] the plains, nets come to be stretched, and horsemen, as men do, come to lead a hunt on the tracks of a wild beast, these are simulations of demons and in no way the truth of things. And if in the rivers (these simulations) come to appear in the form of species of women, these are forms of Satan. Because, there is no crocodile whatsoever that is a personal (being); and a dragon cannot enter into a man, as the demon does, as some have conjectured from the whistling sounds that the possessed person emits; because, for a corporeal (being) into a corporeal (being), there is no means of entering. (Let us add) further that, if (the dragon) comes to rise (into the air), it is not at all with the help of certain animals called oxen, but by the virtue of a certain hidden force, by the order of God, lest its breath should harm a man or an animal. Just as, a species of serpent, which is properly called the basilisk, kills a man or beast merely by looking at them. Hence, when it comes to be found in wells, one descends to the bottom with a lamp in hand to take (it), so that, once it has fixed its gaze on the lamp, the serpent may not go on to harm the man. e. ANGES GARDIENS (développement amené naturellement pour opposer la vérité, bien qu'invisible et mystérieuse, aux croyances chimériques) [I. XXVI] Besides, we, according to the Holy Scriptures, we know that the Angels have as their office [108] to assist men, nations and kingdoms; according to what they say, namely: "He established the boundaries of nations according to the number of the Angels of God" And, again, by what (Jesus) says in the Gospel, namely: "Take heed that ye despise not one of these little ones, because their Angels, perpetually, see the face of my Father, who is in heaven." Thus, in a certain way, it appears that with each man a particular guardian Angel would stand; although others (exegetes) have understood "prayers" by what was said by the Savior; in this sense (361) that their prayers (of the little ones), which perpetually have access before God, are called "Angels". Moreover also the Apostle says: "Are they not all ministering spirits, sent forth to minister for them who shall be heirs of salvation?" they who, for Him, stand in service, and become workers of salvation for us. f. RESPONSE TO AN OBJECTION CONCERNING WHAT HAS BEEN SAID: "If Satan is the instigator of evils, why does he solicit for creatures other than himself the worship of the gentiles?" [I. XXVII] "But if Satan is, they say, the instigator of evils, for others (than himself) among the creatures, why does he solicit (362) [109] the worship of the gentiles, and does he not want to extort it for himself alone?" That Satan has acquired a bad reputation in the matter of wickedness is evident to all! Now if he required this worship for himself alone, he would quickly, quickly frighten away the adherents of his cult, because he would be evil and maleficent in their idea. On the contrary, he has led some to adore luminous bodies, and others, air and fire, earth and water, even ⌈common things⌉ (363): and pieces of wood, and stones, even serpents, and wild beasts, and worms; so that, solely, the jealous envy that he had set in his head, to measure himself in a challenge with man, he might satisfy. And therefore let them not at all go bickering (364), those who adore the creatures, saying that, in their opinion (364), they do not do the will of Satan: for, there was no one else to become a teacher of a worship to be rendered to creatures except Satan alone. And therefore how will they be able to anathematize Satan, those who accomplish his will? For if those who render worship to the true God, and come, on certain points, in their moral conduct, to corrupt themselves, hear themselves, by the divine voice [110], thus inculpated: "They profess to know God, and, by their works, they deny him," how much more are those unable to give an answer to this, who, once they have stolen the worship of the True (God), render (this worship) to inanimate creatures incapable of uttering a cry.

### Conclusion
to 1 (Adversaries in view: the Greeks) and transition to 2 (Adversaries in view: the Persians), even implying, by anticipation, the Second Part, concerning the problem of evil: dualism of Marcion. [I. XXVIII] Only, although, with all sorts of arms the enemy of the truth (365) has in fact equipped himself, by making the Greek Sages believe that there was a certain Matter, standing eternally alongside God, from which he made the creatures, and (although) - because the name for "matter" (ὕλη), in their language, is close to that for "mud" (ἰλύς) - they have for that reason believed that from there the evils had taken their beginning, and (although) the inventors of the religion of the Persians - following the doubts that this question raised in them: "Whence can evils come?", engaging in the same beaten paths, as people led astray (366) far from the truth, the same nonsense, with the help of different stories, have patched up, - as how, [111] by one father, two sons were begotten: a good one, and creator of good things, and an evil one and of evil things, artisan - it is into the same ⌈gropings⌉ (367) that there fell also the heresies (368) that the enemy, like tares among wheat, sowed (369). For some posited three roots (of being): that of the good, and that of the just, and that of the evil; and others, two: that of the good and that of the evil; and some, seven. And therefore, the work of the Church of God, behold it: those from outside, by the truth of things, without (resorting to) Scriptures, (to) refute; and those who, supposedly, are from within, without truly being so, with the help of the Holy Scriptures, (to) refute. The beliefs concerning matter by which the Greeks were led into error, we will consider that, with the refutation of the first dissertations, they have enough for their part (370); and with the inventors of the religion of the Persians, - trust placed in the grace of God - we are going to engage the battle. 2. Adversaries in view: the Persians A. Zrouanism. a. EXPOSITION (371) [113] [II. I] Before anything at all existed, they say, neither heavens, nor earth, nor other creatures whatsoever, that heavens or earth contain, there existed one named Zrouan, (name) which is translated 'Fortune' or 'Glory' (372). For a thousand years, he offered sacrifice to the end that a son might perhaps be born to him, whose name (was to be) Ormizd, who would come to create heavens and earth and all that they contain. Then, after a thousand years of offering sacrifices, he began to roll these words in his mind: "Can there be some use in the sacrifice that I make and can Ormizd be born to me as a son, or is it in pure loss that I am going here giving myself trouble?" While he was making this reflection, Ormizd and Arhmn were conceived in the womb of their mother: Ormizd, fruit of the offering made of sacrifices, [114] and Arhmn, fruit of that doubt. Afterwards, Zrouan, (of the fact), realizes; he says: "Two sons are in that womb; whichever of them, whoever he may be, who quickly comes to me, him I shall make king!" And Ormizd had knowledge of the designs of their father; he revealed (them) to Arhmn, saying: "Zrouan, our father, has formed this design: "Whoever of us, whoever he may be, who ⌈qui⌉ (373) quickly goes to him, him he will make king."" And Ahrmn heard this; he pierced the womb, and came out, stood before his father. And, at the sight of him, Zrouan did not know who he could be; and he asked: "Who are you, you?" And he (Arhmn) said: "I am your son." And Zrouan said to him: <My son! you are not, you!> (374). My son is fragrant and luminous; and you, you are dark and stink!" And while they were exchanging these words among themselves, the birth of Ormizd took place, at his hour; luminous and fragrant he came, stood before Zrouan. And, at the sight of him, Zrouan knew that it was Ormizd his son, for whom he was making the sacrifice; and, once he had taken [115] the twigs that he held in his hand, when (375) he was in the (very) act of sacrifice, he gave (them) to Ormizd, saying: "Until now, it is I who, for you, made sacrifice: henceforth it is you who, for me, will make (it)." And, at the very moment when Zrouan was giving the twigs to Ormizd and blessing him, the approach of Arhmn took place right before Zrouan; he said to him: "Did you not, by vow, in these terms, commit yourself: "Whichever of my two sons, the first, comes to me, him I shall make king?"" And Zrouan, in order not to break the vow, said to Arhmn: "Oh! false and evil-doing! granted be to you kingship for nine thousand years; Ormizd, he (376), over you, sovereign ⌈I have⌉ (377) established; and after nine thousand years Ormizd will reign; and what he will want to create, he will create (it)." It is then that Ormizd and Arhmn set about creating creatures, and everything that Ormizd created was good and right, and everything that Arhmn made was bad and twisted. b. REFUTATION α. Preamble: they say like Mani that they have flayed [II. II] To such incredible words (378) and ravings of besotted minds, [116] stitched together end to end, one should not at all, absolutely, even make a reply; for their very unreason would suffice to refute them (these minds), starting precisely from these same words, which fight one against the other, and contradict one another. Only, since it is by this (unreason) (379), in some way, that the leaders of the said religion appear greatly estimable to their followers, and that once they have thrown a lasso around the body (to them), they drag them to the abyss, necessity imposes itself to give a reply, and to show that they say nothing more than Mani, whom they themselves have flayed. For that one (Mani), it is two roots, that of good and that of evil, that he says (to be); and this, not by conception and birth, but (he says them) subsisting by themselves, contrary to one another. And they, the same thing say (saying, they, that these two roots are produced), by the ardent desires of Zrouan, by conception and by birth. And, if the same religion they have, the ones and the others, why do the Magi come to hate the Zandiks, if not that, by moral conduct, they are separated from one another? Although it be for form and not in reality. But, in religion, the two (groups) are but one: those (the Manichaeans) admit [117] two roots, and these (the Magi) do the same; those render worship to the sun, and these are servants of the sun; in the idea of those all inanimate creatures are animate, and these, in the same way, think the same thing. Only, because Mani wished, with simulations, to make a show of a moral conduct superior to theirs, - namely that he was, if one believed him (380), in everything absolutely free from the passions of concupiscence - and superior not only to theirs, but even to that of all religions, from there came that, proof made against him that he fondled (381) young girls, by death caused by flaying of the skin, he was deprived of life. Conclusion From which it is clear that it is by moral conduct only that they distinguish themselves from one another; because those (the Manichaeans) are false good-men, and these (the Magi) are dissolute, and openly, publicly, taking their ease; but by religious beliefs they are the same and of the same ilk. 1. β. First Refutation [II. III.] For us, let us now leave those (the Manichaeans) aside; let us ask these (the Magi): "That famous Zrouan (382) who they say preexists all things, was he someone perfect? Or imperfect?" If they come to say this (382): "he was perfect", they will hear this: "The Perfect that he was, of whom did he find himself in need to request a son, who [118] would come and create heavens and earth?" For if he was perfect, he himself in person had power to create heavens and earth. Secondly, if he was imperfect, it is clear that there was someone above him, who had power to supply what he lacked in perfection. And if there was someone above him, it is that one who ought to create heavens and earth and all they contain, to manifest his beneficence and power, and not, to Zrouan, by grace, grant a son, who would create heavens and earth and all they contain. But, they say, it is to Glory that he made the sacrifice! We will ask: "Of the said glory, had someone favored him? Or is it because he was eternal, formally (383), that he was glorious?" (384). <If eternal he was, by eternity itself, glorious he was> (385). If it is from someone that this glory had been, as a gift, granted to him, necessity forces us to think that there was someone above him, more powerful and more glorious, from whom the glory came to him. Consequently, if there was no one above him, it was in vain, the sacrifice of a thousand years, to do it! For [119] glory is not a personal being (386), but it is from someone's success that the name of 'glory' comes, just as from someone's failure the name of 'misfortune' comes; and both are 'products of accidents' and not 'hypostases of persons' (387). And yet another thing. For if sun and moon had not yet come into existence, by which hours and days and months and years are regulated, the (lapse of) a thousand years, whence was it to be seen? Since there were not present the luminaries that would have regulated the number of days and months and years (388). It is moreover quite evident that their balderdash is full of folly. Again (an objection): if heavens and earth and what they contain did not exist, where did he make the sacrifice? Or with what? Since the earth did not exist and none of the plants that come out of it, the twigs, whence did he find them, to hold in hand? Or, in sum, what exactly did he sacrifice, since the animals had not yet been created? And what is more than anything mad: "For a thousand years, they say, he made sacrifice; and after [120] thousand years he doubted, saying: "Will a son Ormizd be born to me? or will he not be born? And am I going there giving myself trouble in vain?"" And by this they show that powerless was that famous Zrouan, and in need, and devoid of knowledge! And the cause of evils, it is he himself and not Arhmn! For if he had not doubted, as they rightly say, that famous Arhmn would not have been born, whom they go proclaiming creator of evils. But indeed he himself doubted, <so that not only one son was born of him, but two, one good, because he had made sacrifice, and the other evil, because he had doubted> (389). This is properly (390) unbelievable and full of incoherence. For never from a single source have two shoots come forth, one sweet and the other bitter; nor from a single tree two fruits, one tasty and the other acrid. If therefore, that he was sweet, they know from their Zrouan, they must not hold the bitter fruit, their Arhmn, as issuing from him, and if, as being bitter, they regard it, it is not fitting (390 bis) to posit the sweet fruit, Ormizd, as issuing from him. And aptly comes forth here for them the divine word, which says: "Either make the tree sweet and its fruit sweet; or [121] make the tree bitter and its fruit bitter; for it is from the tree itself that its fruit is recognized" And if (simple) creatures each remain in their composition and never transgress the limits imposed on them, how much more should their Zrouan, if he were someone eternal and sought the means to create creatures, either by himself, or by another, or by his son, as they rightly say, show there a certain order and not disorder! (391) For never did we see cows give birth to donkeys; nor donkeys to oxen; nor wolves to sheep; nor sheep to foxes; nor lions to horses; nor horses to serpents. There is scarcely an exception (392) except one birth that men have found a way to realize outside the laws of nature: to bring forth, from horses and donkeys, mules; and these have no seed, nor do they give birth (to other mules); because in their procreation there was not a realization of the divine plan but a human invention (393). Their Zrouan therefore, supposing he were a bovine, how did he give birth to the scorpion, to their Arhmn? And supposing he were a wolf, [122] why (394) did he give birth to the lamb, to Ormizd? Are these truly balderdash excogitated by men! For their Zrouan too, in fact, he was a man, a valiant male (395) at the time of the Titans, and as Greeks and Arik' and all nations of pagans are accustomed to hold the valiant as sons of gods, with an eye open to this fact, the damer of religion of the Persians (Zoroaster) says to himself (396) (of Zrouan): "Since the men of this country hold him as god, I! will make them believe in the creation of the heavens themselves and earth and all creatures by him!" And that this word is true, it is evident from the fact that it is by bringing into play human moral behaviors that he dams religion and that it is (by linking them) through conception and birth that he sews religious beliefs piece by piece. For, first, of two creators, one good and one evil, issuing from a single father he proclaims the births. And then, by way of shameful commerce with mother and sisters, he introduces the creation of the luminaries. And in doing this he had no other motive than the motive of carnal love and appetitive covetousness. For, with an eye open [123] on the nation of the Arik', seeing the sexual love it has for woman, it is by accommodating himself to these same (397) loose morals which are his own, that he has sewn laws for them piece by piece, so that, when, of their gods, they will come to hear that unworthy beddings they have imagined, they too, resembling (398) these same gods (thereby), might without regard practice the same impudicities. From this Zrouan (399), the divine Being is far removed, He who is in the heights! For, to God, having a son befits not in marriage, but in eternity; as to the intelligence (it befits to have as son) the word; and to the source, the river; and to the fire, the heat; and to the sun, the light. And not as they have the effrontery to say that (this god that was Zrouan), deprived as he was, made vows (400) that a son might be born to him, whose name would be Ormizd! O stupidity! 1. Nowhere yet a son present there; and he, to that one who was not conceived and to that one who was not born, he imposed a name! To all children who are born, it is after their birth that names are imposed. And he, how, before he was born, did he impose a name on him: Ormizd, unless because he believed that in the truth of the fact he too (Ormizd) was born to him as a son (400 bis). 2. And if he [124] believed it, why did he doubt and was he, by this doubt, the cause of the birth of Arhmn, whence evils, in the world, made their entry? 3. And a marvel is it for the occasion (401), that the one, from a thousand years of sacrifices made, just barely was born, and the other, from this instantaneous doubt! 4. Once again, he, who this, knew well, that two sons are there in the womb, of this, why did he not have knowledge, that one (is) good and the other evil? If he knew it well, and did not destroy the evil one, he himself is the cause of evil. Then, if that one (the evil one), he did not know (recognize), how can it be believable that the other (the good one), he had known him (as such)? And if, at that moment, he had not perceived it, at the very moment (402) when, dark and stinking, he saw him, even at that moment, in truth, did he not know (that he was such)? But indeed, and he knew (that he was such) and saw and made the dark one king! And he himself is the cause of evils, for this very reason that he did not destroy the evil one, but, even to kingship, gave him, (kingship) of nine thousand years! And over whom does he establish him king, except over the good creatures, those who held their existence from Ormizd? [125] In order to torture these by mingling with them his own evil creatures! But Ormizd also, they say, he established him king over him. Supposing that the king of that one (Arhmn) is this Ormizd, how can he let his own good creatures be tortured? If the father had no pity (403) on the creatures issued from his son, by the fact that he delivered (them) into the hands of the evil one, how can the son not have compassion on his own (creatures)? (403). The cause? I know not what impotence? or will to do evil? If it is by impotence that he will not have delivered them from danger (404), consequently, there is no, from now on, for him, to reign; and in the end, as they say, he has no power to conquer. But supposing that it is by will to do evil, it turns out that not only the father, who made the evil one king, is cause of evils, but also the son who associated himself with the father's will and gave all freedoms to the evil one. To return (to Zrouan): since the kingship, it was to his sons that he had given it, to one, the (kingship) of nine thousand years, to the other, the indefinite (kingship), himself, in what rank will he go to stand? For as long as nothing existed, over nothing he was [126] king, since indeed he was creator of nothing. And when his sons came into existence, they themselves were creators, one of good (creatures) and the other of evil; and they themselves (were) kings, one for a time, and the other for eternity; and there remained that famous Zrouan, empty-handed of creation and kingship! For, creator he is not, since there was no act of creating on his part; and king he was not, for of which creatures would he have become king? And it is clear that their Zrouan never existed, and that he does not exist at all. For every being that exists, either exists as creator, or as creature. This (being) therefore, since he exists neither as creator nor as creature, never, as they make him (405), was he God; and he is not! and not in the process of becoming! [II. IV] But, when he, Zrouan, they say, conceived this thought in (his) mind: "Whoever, whichever of my sons, quickly comes to me, that one I will make king," Ormizd knew it well (406); indeed, this thought, he revealed (it) to Arhmn. If, of the thought of his father, Ormizd had knowledge, of his evil beast of a brother, the thought, why did he not know it? There it is (407): he [127] pierces the womb and comes out, and takes the lead, usurps the kingship! Which was to be for his misfortune and that of his own creatures! For, first, from the very beginning, once, under his blows, he (Arhmn) will have made him (Ormizd) retreat (408), he (Arhmn) will make him fall from his dignity (of king), and, thereafter (409), for nine thousand years, repentance and regret will there be (for Ormizd), keeping his eyes sadly fixed (410) on his all straight (411) creatures, which, once he has deteriorated them, he (Arhmn) will go render all twisted (412). Or else Zrouan, he, who knew perfectly (413) that it was of two sons that there was conception in a womb, when, once come, this Arhmn stood in his presence, why did he not recognize him? Again, he who, of his son Ormizd, knew that, fragrant and luminous, he was right there in the womb, of his other son, that, stinking and dark, he must (414) be, how did he not know it? Conclusion to β Is it not, in truth, evident that these are not truthful things that are recounted there by them, but patched-up fables! (415) γ. Second Refutation And the other (thing) yet which, of all, is the most incredible, is that the one, from a thousand years of sacrifices made, was just born; and the other, from this instantaneous act [128] of doubting (416). Even if it is by this act of doubting that this Arhmn was engendered as a son, he (Zrouan) should not have called him his son. Because, if he had been his son, similar to himself (Zrouan) in being he would have been and of the same quality: either, supposing that he (Zrouan) were good, good; or, supposing that he were evil, evil. Will one suppose - and in the same way (as they suppose it for Ormizd and Arhmn (417) - that the father, Zrouan, would in reality have been both good and evil? And that it was from his good vein that the good son was engendered, and from his evil vein, the evil one? And if, in a certain way, it had not been so, consequently, he (Zrouan), neither, the evil one, would he have called him his son, nor would he have given him kingship. Moreover, if he himself had been good, he would have destroyed the evil one, and to the good one he would have given the kingship; whereby he himself would have become renowned, and his good son Ormizd would not have made him forever heartbroken. Besides, from all this it appears clearly that there never existed a Zrouan, (who was) father of gods, nor (who was) giver of kingdoms (418). [II. V] They say again: "The twigs that in his hand he held, it is to his son, to Ormizd (the good) that he gave them, saying [129]: "Until now it is I who, for you, made sacrifice; henceforth, it is you who, for me, shall have to (do) it."" Now, suppose (419): he (Zrouan) it was for him (Ormizd) that he made sacrifice, so that, as a son, that one (Ormizd) might be born to him, but Ormizd, for him (Zrouan), for what purposes did he (Ormizd, make sacrifice)? Was it that some mistrust, from somewhere, had come to him (Zrouan), and that, because of this (mistrust), he ordered him (Ormizd), for himself (Zrouan), to make sacrifice? Is it that (the Being) from whom, by request, he had obtained this son, in this way, perhaps, in return for the gift that (this Being) had made him of this son, him (the son), as (his) lot, is he not going to take him? (420). If such a thing had in mind that one (this Being), the twigs could be of no help (421). Then in the very act of giving the twigs, this one (Zrouan) did not say: "You, it is to me that you must make sacrifice", in order to clearly show that there was someone to whom he (Zrouan) owed (to obtain) this son, was making sacrifice; and to this son he ordered, for himself (Zrouan), to that same someone, to make sacrifice (422). And if there existed someone above him and his son, to whom they made the sacrifice, it is that someone that they (the Magi) should know to recognize as being cause of themselves (423) and creator of all, and not Zrouan, as being cause of Ormizd and Arhmn (424), and [130] these, as being creators of evils and goods (425). Rather than giving Zrouan a creator son, He who was above Zrouan, himself, in person, by himself, could he not, heavens and earth and all they contain, create? - as in what precedes (426) it has been said - and his own power and beneficence, (to) show? Or if it is from his own son, from Arhmn (427), that there came (to Zrouan) some cause for mistrust, and if, for that very reason, the twigs, it is to Ormizd that he gave them, so that, sacrifice made with them to the Being who was above them, without mistrust then he might become, certainly, it was necessary that someone existed there present, to whom Ormizd made the sacrifice! And if there existed someone, there present, to whom it was legitimate to make the sacrifice, it follows that Zrouan, he, was not at all eternal, but from someone held his existence. And one must investigate from whom he, precisely, held this existence; and who was the one to whom, he himself, made the sacrifice; and who that one to whom, he (Zrouan) ordered his son to make sacrifice for himself (Zrouan). Because it is not possible for anyone to take beginning in being, if not from another he comes to take being; and there is only God alone who has the power to create [131] from nothing a being, whatever the way he wills it (428). And so, who indeed could be the one who created Zrouan, if not God, to whom also he (Zrouan) was making the sacrifice, who also gave him a son of such a nature that he could create heavens and earth and all that they contain? And it is a marvel that he himself (this God) did not create, and to the son of Zrouan ⌈was given⌉ the power (429) to grant the (ability to) create! "But, to exist, there was no one there present, they say, to whom Zrouan was making the sacrifice." Supposing it to be so, he too, their Zrouan, in reality, did not exist; and the matter deserves a great burst of laughter, that: "He-who-did-not-exist," to "He-who-does-not-exist," for "He-who-does-not-exist," was making sacrifice! [II. VI] But if, also "Fortune" was, as they say, their Zrouan, certainly he was the "fortune" of someone; and who then was the one whose fortune he precisely was? For "fortune" is not a personal being (430), but an accidental result of success. In the way that, from "justice," the "just" is called, and from "valor," a "valiant," in the same way, from that (fortunate) ⌈fortune⌉ (431), a "fortunate one." If therefore "Fortune" was their Zrouan, he was not a "personal being"; whence it is evident [132] that, altogether, he too had no real existence, their Zrouan! And if, as they say, it is by this act of doubting that Arhmn was conceived, it is from the very beginning that he (Zrouan) must have doubted—for, come now! on the spot, immediately a son was born to him—and not, for a thousand years, to weary himself and make sacrifice! For a son, on that account (432), will, for him, come into existence (433), (who will be) either evil or good; and the wickedness, it is from his own moral conduct that he (this son) will take it (433), and not from the fact of that birth (434). Because it was impossible for a single womb to contain the artisan of evils and the creator of goods. For, if it was evil (the womb), of evil only it must be the receptacle; and if it was good, of good. Because the good and the evil could not arrange a place in a single (receptacle), for this reason that wolves and lambs are not born from a single womb (435). If it concerns these (animals), (those that form) the side of good, it is Ormizd that they (the Magi) posit as being their origin; if it concerns then cattle, and sheep, and other useful (animals); and (those that form) the side of evil, it is Arhmn; if it concerns then wolves and wild beasts, and harmful vermin (436). And this they do not know, that just as it is impossible for the harmful, with the [133] non-harmful, to dwell, so also it was not possible for the good with the evil, in a single womb, to be conceived. For, as for example, it is impossible, water and fire, in a single place to unite (437)—in doing so, there are chances that the part which has abounded more becomes destructive of its companion!—just the same equally, for the good and for the evil, in a single place to have access, there was no means. Otherwise, either that one destroyed this one, or this one, that one. Now, supposing that the sons came from a single seed, it was not possible for him (Zruan) alone to ejaculate two seeds, contrary to one another (438). Moreover, it was not possible for one womb to accept two different seeds. For, although many men come together with one woman, nevertheless, in that case, the seeds of all will not go and take hold together (439). For, the seed that falls first (in the womb), the others, as being superfluous, it spews far off. And how would it happen that that womb would accept two seeds, enemies of one another? (440). And once again (441), why did the offspring born from the sacrifice not triumph, and not [134] become an impediment to the offspring born from doubt? Quite the contrary, once descended there, though they were enemies of one another, in good accord, peacefully, they were lying in a single womb! Moreover, if the father knew there were two sons in the womb, one good and the other evil, it was not indiscriminately that he should have promised the kingship, but only to that one for whom he made the sacrifice (443). Not to speak here of Zruan (444), certainly, their Ohrmizd too, before he was born, was not fully developed (445). And how did he, being not fully developed, grasp the paternal thought? For, whoever can know someone's thoughts is above that one; which is proper to God alone, and not to man. From which follows that Ohrmizd is higher than his father both in strength and in intellectual penetration (446). For it was while he was in the womb that, with certain knowledge, he knew his father's thought (447). Then, once having come out of that womb, he had the power to create heavens and earth, which his father had not at all been able to create (448). And now, he who, to this extent, was stronger and more intelligent than his father, finds himself to be (of Arhmn and himself) the one who is worth less; since by the good-for-nothing he let himself be tricked, for he possesses it—the thought [135] of their father—revealed to that one with whom he was to have irreconcilable enmity, and not friendship! To return to it, if there was some obligation (449) to pierce the breast additionally (449) and to come out, it was for that one that there was obligation, the one who had known how to grasp the thought of the father—for, come now! he himself would go first, would take the kingship!—and not at all for Arhmn, who in no way knew (what was) the thought of the father and had, to the kingship, no title! Only, if, in fact, (Arhmn) pierced the breast, perhaps indeed he also really killed the mother! That about which one must inquire: "Truly, was there a mother to exist for them?" But whence can it appear that there was a mother to exist? Especially since they say formally that before anything at all was to be, neither heavens nor earth, Zrouan alone was to be! Which merits a long burst of laughter that he himself could be 'father', and he himself 'mother'; and the same to ejaculate the seed, and the same to receive it! And what again precisely proves that (Zrouan) is worth less (than his sons), (is that) when Arhmn, they say, had pierced the belly and came, stood before the father, not at all did the father recognize him! How [136] then did he not at all recognize him, since no one existed anywhere, during all the time when he himself alone existed? Was it not in fact evident that (the being) who, to (the being he was) himself (450), had come, was some one of his sons? And he (Zrouan) finds himself worth even less than the good-for-nothing! since that one (Arhmn) recognized this one (Zrouan), and this one (Zrouan) not at all recognized that one (Arhmn) and denied that son, saying: "My son is fragrant and luminous; and you, you are dark and stink!" And how was he not at all his son, he who, with his good son, in one same womb, had been conceived? And, regarding that one (Arhmn), he denied him, saying: "You, you are not my son!" and, regarding the other, he affirmed saying: "He is my son!" And if it is as wicked that he denied him, consequently, from the very conception he ought not to have judged him worthy, but straightway, wicked as he was, to have him in horror and slay him, and not only him, but also Ormizd, who had disclosed (the secret of) his thoughts to him (Zrouan). [II. VII] But the other thing also even more makes no sense, which they expressly say, namely: "And the sticks he gave him to make sacrifice for himself (Zrouan)", as if not in Ormizd or in the rites of sacrifice was the (imperative) power [137] but in the sticks! For, if he himself (Ormizd) had claims to be heard, consequently it was a superfluous thing to hold the sticks in hand! And if he himself was not worthy (of being heard), neither were the sticks sufficient to render worthy of ritually sacrificing, the one who was not worthy (of being heard). For holding sticks and making sacrifice is the work of men and not of God; <and to hear, is it not the work of men, but of God> (451). In fact, if he (Ormizd) was God and had power to create heavens and earth, what need had he to hold sticks and make sacrifice, to draw his father from the perils of which he was wary? He who had power to create heavens and earth without the sticks, his father, how could he not render him without worries, without the sticks? And now (452) it has become evident that the father was deprived of spirit (453) and powerless and forced to have recourse to another (than himself); and the son likewise powerless and deprived of spirit. For, neither that one (the father), without making sacrifice, could beget a son; nor the son, without receiving the sticks in hand, could free him (the father) from (his) wariness. [II. VIII] Moreover, of the vexations also of the evil one, [138] both were causes, of the tortures he inflicted on the good creatures of the good one. For Ormizd, they say, all that was good, that is what he created: both just men and doing good; and Arhmn (created) the evil creatures and the devils. If therefore the devils were creatures of an evil one, and (themselves) evil by nature, not even a single one among them, whoever he might be, of a good thing, could ever have the idea; much less, nor Arhmn in person, of his own accord. Only, at present, we see that "the only one who is in a truly sovereign manner full of charm among creatures", as they say (454), it is Arhmn who found a way to bring her into existence. When he saw, they say, that Ormizd had created beautiful creatures, and that light he had not known how to create, he took counsel with devils, saying: "What use is it to Ormizd, to have created such beautiful creatures? And in darkness they remain, because light he has not known how to create! Now, if he were intelligent, with his mother he would have intercourse (455), and the sun, as a son, would be born; and upon his sister, he would fall, and the moon he would beget!" And he gave order that no one should go [139] to divulge the counsel. That, Mahmi, a devil, heard it; quickly quickly to Ormizd he came, and the counsel, in his ear, he divulged! O foolishness and insipid stupidity! He who could well find the way to create heavens and earth and all they contain, the minute thing that was the cunning to put into the aforementioned invention, he would have been incapable of conceiving it! (456). And thereby, not only do they make (him) Ormizd devoid of intelligence, but (they make) also Arhmn the inventor of the best creatures! As, moreover, yet another thing that they say (457), namely that this famous Arhmn says: "It is not that I cannot create a good being (458), but I do not want to"; and to confirm the word, he created the peacock. You see that it is by will that he is evil, and not at all by birth! Now what can there be more resplendent than light, of which Arhmn was the inventor, or than (can there be) anything more beautiful than the peacock, which, to prove the faculty he had of creating beauty, he created? And by that it appears that if Arhmn had been evil by nature, he would [140] not at all have been the inventor of light, nor creator of beauty. Furthermore, if devils had been evil by nature, there would have been no means for Mahmi to become the announcer of the manner in which one must proceed to create light; (this Mahmi) to whom, up to the present, the ministers of this religion, three times a year, offer victims! Hence, once they have (thus) refuted themselves (459), they are reproached (with reason) for being themselves also ministers of devils and, for the devils, for not admitting that (460), evil, they are not so by nature, but by will. Then, if they themselves offer a victim to a devil, with what face will the ministers of devils go to persecute them? Do you see how everything, down to the detail, of what is said by them, is fables and hollow tales? [II. IX] Moreover, they themselves, who, for the creation of the luminaries, posit as a thesis that it owes its origin to similar causes, contrary to these assertions (461), introduce a slightly different cause for the coming into existence of the sun. Arhmn, they say, invited Ormizd to a meal; and, once he came, Ormizd did not want to eat the meal until their sons fought.
